61.31784pt\raise-15.68056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.56786pt\raise-15.68056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\gamma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.56786pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.60167pt\raise-31.36111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\sigma}{\mathbf{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.72394pt\raise-37.86111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\sigma}(\kappa^{n})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.96619pt\raise-31.36111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.96619pt\raise-31.36111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\sigma}{\mathbf{U}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Expanding the definition (
6.4
) of
\kappa^{n}
,
the definition (
4.10
) of the Segal–Becker splitting
c
,
and exploiting that
\Omega^{\bullet}(\eta)\circ c=\Theta
by Theorem
4.12

shows that the
following diagram commutes in the unstable
C
-global homotopy category:

(6.7)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.5764pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.44437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{BUP}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.36711pt\raise 5.5853pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Upsilon^{n}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 182.33276pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.57375pt\raise-15.84027pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{c}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 97.36775pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 182.33276pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{BUP}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 197.77713pt\raise-15.84027pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Theta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 185.52711pt\raise-15.84027pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 197.77713pt\raise-23.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-24.5764pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}{\mathbf{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.75122pt\raise-38.33878pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.82487pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\phi^{n})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.79135pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 75.79135pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}{\mathbf{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.42204pt\raise-38.2971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.86655pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}(\eta)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 176.1591pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 176.1591pt\raise-31.68056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathbf{KR}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Clearly, the morphism
\phi^{1}
is the identity of
{\mathbf{P}}
,

\kappa^{1}
is the identity of
{\mathbf{U}}
, and
\Upsilon^{1}
is the identity of
{\mathbf{BUP}}
.

The next proposition verifies a globally-coherent version
of the design criterion for Adams operations, namely that on line bundles,

\psi^{n}
is the
n
-th tensor power.
The morphism of
C
-global spaces
h\colon{\mathbf{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{BUP}}
was defined in (
4.15
);
it represents the inclusion of line bundles into virtual vector bundles.

Proposition 6.8
.

For every
n\geq 1
, the following diagram commutes
in the unstable
C
-global homotopy category:

\textstyle{{\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{n}}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{BUP}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon^{n}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{BUP}}

Proof.

Corollary
4.16
shows that
c\circ h\colon{\mathbf{P}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}{\mathbf{P}})

is the unit of the adjunction
(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+},\Omega^{\bullet})
;
naturality of the adjunction unit yields

(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\phi^{n})\circ c\circ h=c\circ h\circ\phi^{n}
.
Hence

\Theta\circ\Upsilon^{n}\circ h\ =_{\eqref{eq:Upsilon_and_c}}\ \Omega^{\bullet}(\eta)\circ(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\phi^{n})\circ c\circ h\ =\ \Omega^{\bullet}(\eta)\circ c\circ h\circ\phi^{n}\ =\ \Theta\circ h\circ\phi^{n}\ .

The final equation is Theorem
4.12
.
Since
\Theta\colon{\mathbf{BUP}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathbf{KR}})

is a
C
-global equivalence by Theorem
B.52
(i), we can cancel it,
and deduce the desired relation.
∎

The next theorem justifies the name ‘global Adams operation’
for the morphism
\Upsilon^{n}\colon{\mathbf{BUP}}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{BUP}}
.

Theorem 6.9
.

For every augmented Lie group
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
, every finite
G
-CW-complex
A

and every
n\geq 1
, the following square commutes:

\textstyle{[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon^{n}_{*}}
(
B.10
)
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
B.10
)
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{KR_{\alpha}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{n}}
\textstyle{KR_{\alpha}(A)}

Proof.

We define a map

\delta_{\alpha,A}\ :\ [A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ KR_{\alpha}(A)\text{\quad by\quad}\delta_{\alpha,A}(x)\ =\ \psi^{n}\langle x\rangle-\langle\Upsilon^{n}_{*}(x)\rangle\ ,

the difference of the two composites around the diagram in question.
We need to show that
\delta_{\alpha,A}=0
for all
(\alpha,A)
.

We claim that these maps satisfy:

(a)

The maps
\delta_{\alpha,A}
are natural for continuous
G
-maps in
A
,
and for morphisms of augmented Lie groups.

(b)

The map
\delta_{\alpha,A}
satisfies
\delta_{\alpha,A}(2x-y)=2\cdot\delta_{\alpha,A}(x)-\delta_{\alpha,A}(y)

for all
x,y\in[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}
.

(c)

The map
\delta_{\alpha,A}
is additive whenever the augmentation
\alpha

is trivial.

Property (a) is clear because the Adams operations
and the isomorphisms (
B.10
) are natural in
A
and
\alpha
,
and so are the maps
\Upsilon^{n}_{*}
, since they arise from a morphism
of Real-global spaces.

(b)
Applying
[A,-]^{\alpha}
to the defining diagram
for
\Upsilon^{n}
yields a commutative diagram

\textstyle{[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon^{n}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[A,\Omega^{\sigma}{\mathbf{U}}]^{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Omega^{\sigma}(\kappa^{n}))_{*}}
\textstyle{[A,\Omega^{\sigma}{\mathbf{U}}]^{\alpha}}

in which all objects are abelian groups, but the horizontal maps are not a priori additive.
The vertical maps are isomorphisms of abelian groups because
\gamma
is a
C
-global
equivalence of ultra-commutative
C
-monoids.
As induced by a
\sigma
-loop map,
the map
(\Omega^{\sigma}(\kappa^{n}))_{*}
satisfies

(\Omega^{\sigma}(\kappa^{n}))_{*}(2x-y)\ =\ 2\cdot(\Omega^{\sigma}(\kappa^{n}))_{*}(x)\ -\ (\Omega^{\sigma}(\kappa^{n}))_{*}(y)

for all
x,y\in[A,\Omega^{\sigma}{\mathbf{U}}]^{\alpha}
, see Theorem
4.20
(i).
So the upper horizontal map
\Upsilon_{*}^{n}
satisfies the analogous relation

\Upsilon^{n}_{*}(2x-y)\ =\ 2\cdot\Upsilon^{n}_{*}(x)-\Upsilon^{n}_{*}(y)

for all
x,y\in[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}
.
The Adams operation
\psi^{n}
is additive, so the difference

\delta_{\alpha,A}
still has the weak additivity property (b).

(c) The ‘non-Real’ global morphism underlying
\Upsilon^{n}
is a global loop
map, by construction. So for all compact Lie groups
G
with trivial augmentation,
the map
\Upsilon^{n}_{*}\colon[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{G}\longrightarrow[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{G}
is additive.
The Adams operation
\psi^{n}
is additive, too, hence so is the difference
\delta_{\alpha,A}
.

Now we show in five steps that the maps
\delta_{\alpha,A}
vanish.
The first step is
(\alpha,A)=(\tilde{T},\ast)
and the class of the tautological
Real
\tilde{T}
-representation.
By definition, the morphism
h\colon{\mathbf{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{BUP}}
satisfies

i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\}=h_{*}(u_{\tilde{T}})
in
\pi_{0}^{\tilde{T}}({\mathbf{BUP}})
. So

\displaystyle\langle\Upsilon^{n}_{*}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\})\rangle

\displaystyle=\ \langle\Upsilon^{n}_{*}(h_{*}(u_{\tilde{T}}))\rangle\ =\ \langle h_{*}(\phi^{n}_{*}(u_{\tilde{T}}))\rangle\ =\ \langle h_{*}(\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{*}(u_{\tilde{T}}))\rangle

\displaystyle=\ \langle\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{*}(h_{*}(u_{\tilde{T}}))\rangle\ =\ \langle\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{*}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\})\rangle\ =\ \tilde{\mu}_{n}^{*}\langle i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\}\rangle\ =\ \tilde{\mu}_{n}^{*}[\nu_{1}]\ =\ \psi^{n}[\nu_{1}]\ =\ \psi^{n}\langle i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\}\rangle\ .

The second equation is Proposition
6.8
.
This relation precisely means that
\delta_{\tilde{T},\ast}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\})=0
.

The second step deals with
the tautological Real
\tilde{U}(k)
-representation
\nu_{k}
.
For
1\leq i\leq k
, we let
p_{i}\colon T^{k}\longrightarrow\tilde{T}

denote the projection to the
i
-th factor,
followed by the inclusion
T\longrightarrow\tilde{T}
into the extended circle group. Then

\displaystyle\res^{\tilde{U}(k)}_{T^{k}}(\delta_{\tilde{U}(k),\ast}(i_{*}\{\nu_{k}\}))\

\displaystyle=\ \delta_{T^{k},\ast}(\res^{\tilde{U}(k)}_{T^{k}}(i_{*}\{\nu_{k}\}))\ =\ \delta_{T^{k},\ast}(p_{1}^{*}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\})+\dots+p_{k}^{*}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\}))

\displaystyle=\ \sum_{1\leq i\leq k}\delta_{T^{k},\ast}(p_{i}^{*}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\}))\ =\ \sum_{1\leq i\leq k}p_{i}^{*}(\res^{\tilde{T}}_{T}(\delta_{\tilde{T},\ast}(i_{*}\{\nu_{1}\})))\ =\ 0\ .

The first and forth equation are the naturality property (a)
in the group.
The third equation uses that the map
\delta_{T^{k},\ast}
is additive by (c),
because
T^{k}
is trivially augmented.
The restriction homomorphism
\res^{\tilde{U}(k)}_{U(k)}\colon RR(\tilde{U}(k))\longrightarrow R(U(k))

is injective, see for example
[
4
, page 13]
.
And the restriction homomorphism
\res^{U(k)}_{T^{k}}\colon R(U(k))\longrightarrow R(T^{k})

is injective, too.
So this proves that
\delta_{\tilde{U}(k),\ast}(i_{*}\{\nu_{k}\})=0

in
RR(\tilde{U}(k))
, the Real representation ring of
\tilde{U}(k)
.

In the third step we fix
k\geq 0
, and we write
j_{\alpha,A}^{k}
for the composite

[A,\mathbf{Gr}_{k}]^{\alpha}\ \xrightarrow{\ i^{k}_{*}\ }\ [A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ \xrightarrow{\delta_{\alpha,A}}\ KR_{\alpha}(A)\ \xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:BUP_to_KR_alpha}^{-1}}\ [A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}\ .

Here
i^{k}\colon\mathbf{Gr}_{k}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{BUP}}
is the restriction of
the morphism
i\colon\mathbf{Gr}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{BUP}}
to the
k
-th summand.
Varying
\alpha
and
A
yields a
C
-global transformation

j^{k}
from
\mathbf{Gr}_{k}
to
{\mathbf{BUP}}
in the sense of Definition
A.9
.
The orthogonal
C
-space
\mathbf{Gr}_{k}
is a
C
-global classifying space
for the augmented Lie group
\tilde{U}(k)
, and the class
\{\nu_{k}\}

is the tautological class
u_{\tilde{U}(k),\ast}
in the sense of
(
A.11
).
We showed in the previous step that this
C
-global transformation vanishes
on the tautological class;
so
j_{\alpha,A}^{k}
vanishes on all elements of
[A,\mathbf{Gr}_{k}]^{\alpha}
, for all augmented Lie groups
and all finite equivariant CW-complexes, by
the uniqueness part of Theorem
A.12
.

In the fourth step we assume that
A
is
G
-connected, i.e.,
the group
\pi_{0}(G)
acts transitively on
\pi_{0}(A)
.
This ensures that every Real
\alpha
-vector bundle over
A
has constant rank.
By the isomorphism of Theorem
B.12
(iii),
every element of
[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}
is thus of the form
j_{\alpha,A}^{k}(y)-j_{\alpha,A}^{l}(z)

for some
k,l\geq 0
, some
y\in[A,\mathbf{Gr}_{k}]^{\alpha}
and some

z\in[A,\mathbf{Gr}_{l}]^{\alpha}
.
Then

\displaystyle\delta_{\alpha,A}(j_{\alpha,A}^{k}(y)-j_{\alpha,A}^{l}(z))\

\displaystyle=\ \delta_{\alpha,A}(2\cdot j_{\alpha,A}^{k}(y)-j_{\alpha,A}^{k+l}(y+z))

{}_{\text{(b)}}

\displaystyle=\ 2\cdot\delta_{\alpha,A}(j_{\alpha,A}^{k}(y))\ -\ \delta_{\alpha,A}(j_{\alpha,A}^{k+l}(y+z))\ =\ 0\ .

The third equality is step three.

In the fifth and final step we let
A
be any finite
G
-CW-complex.
Then
A=A_{1}\amalg\ldots\amalg A_{m}
is a disjoint union of
G
-connected
finite
G
-CW-complexes, indexed by the
\pi_{0}(G)
-orbits of
\pi_{0}(A)
.
We write
\iota_{j}\colon A_{j}\longrightarrow A
for the inclusion of the
j
-th summand.
Then by step four,

\iota_{j}^{*}(\delta_{\alpha,A}(x))\ =\ \delta_{\alpha,A_{j}}(\iota_{j}^{*}(x))\ =\ 0

for all
1\leq j\leq m
and every
x\in[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}
.
Real-equivariant K-theory takes disjoint unions to products, so the map

(\iota_{1}^{*},\dots,\iota_{m}^{*})\ :\ KR_{\alpha}(A)\ \longrightarrow\ KR_{\alpha}(A_{1})\times\dots\times KR_{\alpha}(A_{m})

is an isomorphism of rings. Hence
\delta_{\alpha,A}(x)=0
, which concludes the proof.
∎

The Adams operations satisfy
\psi^{m}\circ\psi^{n}=\psi^{mn}
for all
m,n\geq 1
.
So by Theorem
6.9
, the morphisms of Real-global spaces

\Upsilon^{m}\circ\Upsilon^{n},\ \Upsilon^{mn}\ :\ {\mathbf{BUP}}\ \longrightarrow\ {\mathbf{BUP}}

induce the same map on
[A,{\mathbf{BUP}}]^{\alpha}
for all augmented Lie groups
and all finite equivariant CW-complexes.
I do not know if in fact
\Upsilon^{m}\circ\Upsilon^{n}
equals
\Upsilon^{mn}
as endomorphisms
of
{\mathbf{BUP}}
in the unstable
C
-global homotopy category.
Or even better, if the
\sigma
-deloopings (
6.4
)
of the global Adams operations satisfy
\kappa^{m}\circ\kappa^{n}=\kappa^{mn}\colon{\mathbf{U}}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{U}}
.
Also,
\Upsilon^{n}
induces additive maps upon applying
[A,-]^{\alpha}

for all augmented Lie groups and all finite equivariant CW-complexes;
a natural question is thus whether
\Upsilon^{n}
is a
C
-global H-map.
I expect this to be the case, but our techniques do not suffice to show it.

Appendix A
Some
C
-global homotopy theory

In this appendix we develop some basics about
C
-global homotopy theory that we need in
this paper.
In the bulk of the article, the group
C
is the Galois group of
{\mathbb{C}}
over
{\mathbb{R}}
;
the arguments in this appendix work more generally,
and here we let
C
be any compact Lie group.
The main results of this appendix are the classification of
C
-global transformations
with source a
C
-global classifying space in Theorem
A.12
,
and the criterion of Proposition
A.17

to recognize coinduced
C
-spaces.
Two other references that develop general
C
-global homotopy theory are

[
25
, Appendix A]
and
[
5
]
.

An
inner product space
is a finite-dimensional real vector space equipped
with a scalar product, i.e., a positive-definite symmetric bilinear form.
We denote by
{\mathbf{L}}
the topological category with objects the inner product spaces and morphisms the
linear isometric embeddings, topologized as Stiefel manifolds.

Definition A.1
.

Let
C
be a compact Lie group. An
orthogonal
C
-space
is a continuous functor
from the linear isometries category
{\mathbf{L}}
to the category of
C
-spaces.

The notion of
C
-global equivalence
for morphisms of
orthogonal
C
-spaces is defined in
[
25
, Definition A.2]

and
[
5
, Definition 3.2]
.
It generalizes that of global equivalences of orthogonal spaces
from
[
24
, Definition 1.1.2]
, to which it reduces when
C
is the trivial group.
The
C
-global equivalences are part of the
C
-global model structure

on the category of orthogonal
C
-spaces established in
[
5
, Theorem A.20]
.
When
C
is a trivial group, this specializes to the global model
structure on orthogonal spaces from
[
24
, Theorem 1.2.21]
.

Construction A.2

(Equivariant homotopy sets)
.

We introduce the equivariant homotopy sets defined by orthogonal
C
-spaces,
for a compact Lie group
C
. When
C
is the trivial group,
these are discussed in more detail in
[
24
, Section 1.5]
.

For every compact Lie group
G
, we choose a complete
G
-universe, i.e.,
an orthogonal
G
-representation
{\mathcal{U}}_{G}
of countably infinite dimension
such that every finite-dimensional
G
-representation embeds into
{\mathcal{U}}_{G}

by an
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear
G
-equivariant isometric embedding.
We write
s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
for the poset, under inclusion, of finite-dimensional

G
-subrepresentations of
{\mathcal{U}}_{G}
.
We let
E
be an orthogonal
C
-space, we let
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
be a
continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups.
For every
V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
, the space

E(V)
becomes a
(C\times G)
-space via the
C
-action on
E
,
and the
G
-action on
V
through the functoriality of
E
.
We write
\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))
for the
G
-space obtained by
restriction of scalars along
(\alpha,\Id)\colon G\longrightarrow C\times G
.
Said differently,
G
acts diagonally on
\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))
,
via the
G
-action on
E
through
\alpha
, and on
V
.
For a
G
-space
A
, we set

(A.3)

[A,E]^{\alpha}\ =\ \colim_{V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})}\,[A,\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))]^{G}\ .

Here
[-,-]^{G}
is the set of
G
-equivariant
homotopy classes of equivariant maps.
The colimit is taken along the maps
E(V)\longrightarrow E(W)
induced by the
inclusions for
V\subseteq W
in
s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
.

We shall also use the notation

\pi_{0}^{\alpha}(E)\ =\ \colim_{V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})}\,\pi_{0}(\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))^{G})\ .

Evaluation at the unique point is a natural bijection

[\ast,\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))]^{G}\cong\pi_{0}(\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))^{G})
; in the colimit over
the poset
s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
, this becomes a natural bijection

[\ast,E]^{\alpha}\ \cong\ \pi_{0}^{\alpha}(E)\ .

In the following we shall routinely identify

[\ast,E]^{\alpha}
and
\pi_{0}^{\alpha}(E)
in this way without further notice.

The sets
[A,E]^{\alpha}
are contravariantly functorial
for continuous
G
-maps in
A
by precomposition, and covariantly functorial
for morphisms of orthogonal
C
-spaces in
E
. Another functoriality
in
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
will be discussed in Construction
A.5
below.

When
C
is the trivial group, the following proposition specializes to

[
24
, Proposition 1.5.3]
.
The proof in the present
C
-global context is almost literally the same,
so we omit it.

Proposition A.4
.

Let
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
be a continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups,
let
E
be an orthogonal
C
-space, and let
A
be a
G
-space.

(i)

Suppose that the
G
-space
A
is compact and the orthogonal space underlying
E

is closed, i.e., all structure maps
E(\psi)\colon E(V)\longrightarrow E(W)
are closed embeddings.
Then the canonical map

[A,E]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [A,\alpha^{\flat}(E({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))]^{G}

is bijective, where
E({\mathcal{U}}_{G})=\colim_{V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})}E(V)
.

(ii)

If
A
is a finite
G
-CW-complex, then the assignment
E\mapsto[A,E]^{\alpha}

sends
C
-global equivalences of orthogonal
C
-spaces to bijections.

(iii)

If
F
is another orthogonal
C
-space, then the map

([A,p_{E}],[A,p_{F}])\ :\ [A,E\times F]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [A,E]^{\alpha}\times[A,F]^{\alpha}

is bijective, where
p_{E}
and
p_{F}
are the projections.

Construction A.5

(Functoriality in the group)
.

Let
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
be a continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups,
and let
E
be an orthogonal
C
-space.
A continuous homomorphism
\beta\colon K\longrightarrow G
of compact
Lie groups induces a restriction map

(A.6)

\beta^{*}\ :\ [A,E]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [\beta^{*}(A),E]^{\alpha\beta}\ ,

as follows.
Given
V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
and a continuous
G
-equivariant map

f\colon A\longrightarrow\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))
, restriction of actions along
\beta

yields a
K
-equivariant map

\beta^{*}(f)\ :\ \beta^{*}(A)\ \longrightarrow\ \beta^{*}(\alpha^{\flat}(E(V)))\ =\ (\alpha\beta)^{\flat}(E(\beta^{*}(V)))\ .

We choose a
K
-equivariant linear isometric embedding

j\colon\beta^{*}(V)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}_{K}
into the chosen
K
-universe.
Then
W=j(\beta^{*}(V))
is a finite-dimensional
K
-subrepresentation,
and thus an element of the poset
s({\mathcal{U}}_{K})
; and the embedding
j
restricts
to an isomorphism of
K
-representations
j\colon\beta^{*}(V)\cong W
.
The restriction map (
A.6
) sends the class represented by
f
in

[A,E]^{\alpha}
to the class in
[\beta^{*}(A),E]^{\alpha\beta}
represented by the composite

K
-equivariant map

\beta^{*}(A)\ \xrightarrow{\beta^{*}(f)}\ (\alpha\beta)^{\flat}(E(\beta^{*}(V)))\ \xrightarrow[\cong]{\ E(j)\ }\ (\alpha\beta)^{\flat}(E(W))\ .

The analogous argument as in the special case
C=\{1\}
and
A=\ast

in
[
24
, Proposition 1.5.8]

shows that the resulting class in
[\beta^{*}(A),E]^{\alpha\beta}

does not depend on the choice of embedding
j\colon\beta^{*}(V)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}_{K}
,
and so the construction is well-defined.
Given well-definedness, the construction is clearly natural in
A
and
E
,
and contravariantly functorial in groups augmented to
C
:
given another continuous homomorphism
\gamma\colon L\longrightarrow K
, we have

\gamma^{*}\circ\beta^{*}\ =\ (\beta\circ\gamma)^{*}\ :\ [A,E]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [(\beta\gamma)^{*}(A),E]^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ .

Construction A.7

(Induction isomorphisms)
.

We let
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
be a continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups,
we let
E
be an orthogonal
C
-space, and we let
H
be a closed subgroup of
G
.
For an
H
-space
B
, we write

[1,-]\ :\ B\ \longrightarrow\ G\times_{H}B\ ,\quad y\ \longmapsto\ [1,y]

for the
H
-equivariant unit of the adjunction
(G\times_{H}-,\res^{G}_{H})
.
The adjunction bijections

[G\times_{H}B,\alpha^{\flat}(E(V))]^{G}\ \cong\ [B,(\alpha|_{H})^{\flat}(E(V))]^{H}\ ,\quad[f]\ \longmapsto\ [\res^{G}_{H}(f)\circ[1,-]]

for
V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
, and the fact that the underlying
H
-universe of
{\mathcal{U}}_{G}
is a
complete
H
-universe provide an
induction isomorphism
:
the composite

(A.8)

[G\times_{H}B,E]^{\alpha}\ \xrightarrow{\res^{G}_{H}}\ [G\times_{H}B,E]^{\alpha|_{H}}\ \xrightarrow{[1,-]^{*}}\ [B,E]^{\alpha|_{H}}

is bijective, where
\res^{G}_{H}
is short hand for the restriction homomorphism
(
A.6
) associated with the inclusion
H\longrightarrow G
.

If
H
is a closed subgroup of smaller dimension in a compact Lie group
G
,
then the underlying
H
-space of a
G
-CW-complex
need not admit an
H
-CW-structure;
an example is given in
[
13
, Section 2]
.
Nevertheless, the underlying
H
-space of a finite
G
-CW-complex
is always
H
-homotopy equivalent to a finite
H
-CW-complex,
see
[
13
, Corollary B]
.
Consequently, for every continuous homomorphism
\beta\colon K\longrightarrow G
of
compact Lie groups, the restriction functor
\beta^{*}
takes

G
-spaces of the
G
-homotopy type of a finite
G
-CW-complex to

K
-spaces of the
K
-homotopy type of a finite
K
-CW-complex.

Definition A.9

(
C
-global transformations)
.

Let
C
be a compact Lie group, and let
F
and
E
be orthogonal
C
-spaces.
A
C
-global transformation

\tau
from
F
to
E
consists of maps

\tau_{\alpha,A}\ :\ [A,F]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [A,E]^{\alpha}

for all continuous homomorphism
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
of compact Lie groups
and all
G
-spaces
A
of the
G
-homotopy type of a finite
G
-CW-complex that are
natural in the following sense:

•

For every continuous
G
-map
f\colon B\longrightarrow A
, we have

\tau_{\alpha,B}\circ[f,F]^{\alpha}\ =\ [f,E]^{\alpha}\circ\tau_{\alpha,A}\ :\ [A,F]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [B,E]^{\alpha}\ .

•

For every continuous homomorphism
\beta\colon K\longrightarrow G
of compact Lie groups, we have

\tau_{\alpha\beta,\beta^{*}(A)}\circ\beta^{*}\ =\ \beta^{*}\circ\tau_{\alpha,A}\ :\ [A,F]^{\alpha}\ \longrightarrow\ [\beta^{*}(A),E]^{\alpha\beta}\ .

Construction A.10

(Global classifying spaces)
.

We let
\beta\colon K\longrightarrow C
be a continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups.
We choose a faithful
K
-representation
V
and define the

C
-global classifying space
as the orthogonal
C
-space

B_{\gl}\beta\ =\ C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,-)\ .

In more detail,
(B_{\gl}\beta)(W)
is the quotient of the
C
-space

C\times{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)
by the equivalence relation

(c,\varphi)\ \sim\ (c\cdot\beta(k),\varphi\circ l_{k})

for all
(c,k)\in C\times K
and all linear isometric embeddings

\varphi\colon V\longrightarrow W
, where
l_{k}\colon V\longrightarrow V
is the action of
k\in K
.
The notation
B_{\gl}\beta
is slightly abusive in that we do not record the choice of
faithful
K
-representation. This abuse is justified by

[
25
, Proposition A.5]
, showing that

B_{\gl}\beta
is independent of the choice of faithful representation
up to a preferred zigzag of
C
-global equivalences.

The equivalence class

[1,\Id_{V}]\ \in\ C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,V)\ =\ (B_{\gl}\beta)(V)

is a
K
-fixed point of
\beta^{\flat}((B_{\gl}\beta)(V))
, so it represents a class

(A.11)

u_{\beta}\ \in\ \pi_{0}^{\beta}(B_{\gl}\beta)\ ,

the
tautological class
.

Theorem A.12
.

Let
\beta\colon K\longrightarrow C
be a continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups,
and let
E
be an orthogonal
C
-space.
For every class
y
in
\pi_{0}^{\beta}(E)
, there is a unique
C
-global
transformation
\tau
from
B_{\gl}\beta
to
E
such that the map

\tau_{\beta,\ast}\ :\ \pi_{0}^{\beta}(B_{\gl}\beta)\ \longrightarrow\ \pi_{0}^{\beta}(E)

sends the tautological class
u_{\beta}
to
y
.

Proof.

To construct
\tau
we represent
y
by a
K
-fixed point

\tilde{y}\in(\beta^{\flat}(E(W))^{K}
,
for some
K
-representation
W
.
By enlarging
W
, if necessary, we can assume that there is a
K
-equivariant
linear isometric embedding
\varphi\colon V\longrightarrow W
from the faithful
K
-representation
that is implicit in the construction of
B_{\gl}\beta
.
By
[
25
, Proposition A.5]
,
the morphism of orthogonal
C
-spaces

\varphi^{*}=C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(\varphi,-)\ :\ Y=C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(W,-)\ \longrightarrow\ C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,-)\ =\ B_{\gl}\beta

is a
C
-global equivalence.
Moreover, if we let
\llbracket 1,\Id_{W}\rrbracket\in\pi_{0}^{\beta}(Y)
denote the class
represented by the
K
-fixed point

[1,\Id_{W}]
of
\beta^{\flat}(C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(W,W))=\beta^{\flat}(Y(W))
,
then
\pi_{0}^{\beta}(\varphi^{*})\llbracket 1,\Id_{W}\rrbracket=u_{\beta}
.

The fixed point
\tilde{y}
is represented by a unique morphism
of orthogonal
C
-spaces
f\colon Y\longrightarrow E

such that the map

f(W)\ :\ C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(W,W)=Y(W)\ \longrightarrow\ E(W)

takes
[1,\Id_{W}]
to
\tilde{y}
.
The global equivalence
\varphi^{*}
and the morphism
f
induce a
C
-global
transformation
\tau
from
B_{\gl}\beta
to
E
with constituents

\tau_{\alpha,A}\ :\ [A,B_{\gl}\beta]^{\alpha}\ \xrightarrow[\cong]{([A,\varphi^{*}]^{\alpha})^{-1}}\ [A,Y]^{\alpha}\ \xrightarrow{[A,f]^{\alpha}}\ [A,E]^{\alpha}\ .

This
C
-global transformation satisfies

\tau_{\beta,\ast}(u_{\beta})\ =\ \pi_{0}^{\beta}(f)(\pi_{0}^{\beta}(\varphi^{*})^{-1}(u_{\beta}))\ =\ \pi_{0}^{\beta}(f)\llbracket 1,\Id_{W}\rrbracket\ =\ [\tilde{y}]\ =\ y\ .

The proof of uniqueness is more involved.
We let
V
be the faithful
K
-representation implicit in the
definition of
B_{\gl}\beta
.
We let
W
be an inner product space.
The group
C\times O(W)
acts on

(B_{\gl}\beta)(W)=C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)
by

(c,A)\cdot[d,\varphi]\ =\ [cd,A\circ\varphi]\ .

This action is transitive;
and for a linear isometric embedding
\varphi\colon V\longrightarrow W
,
the stabilizer of the point
[1,\varphi]\in C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)
is the subgroup

{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]\ =\ \{(c,A)\in C\times O(W)\colon\text{there is $k\in K$ such that $c=\beta(k)$ and $A\varphi=\varphi l_{k}$}\}\ .

Since the
K
-action on
V
is faithful, for every
(c,A)\in{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]
,
the
k\in K
such that
c=\beta(k)
and
A\varphi=\varphi l_{k}
is unique,
and we can define a continuous
homomorphism
\gamma\colon{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]\longrightarrow K
by letting
\gamma(c,A)
be this
unique element
k
of
K
.
We let
\Pi\colon C\times O(W)\longrightarrow C
denote the projection to the first factor.
Then the square of continuous group homomorphisms

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
incl
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{C\times O(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{C}

commutes by design.
Since the map

(C\times O(W))/{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]\ \longrightarrow\ C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)=(B_{\gl}\beta)(W)\ ,\quad[c,A]\ \longmapsto\ [c,A\varphi]

is a
(C\times O(W))
-equivariant homeomorphism,
the induction isomorphism (
A.8
) shows that the composite

[C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W),E]^{\Pi}\ \xrightarrow{\res^{C\times O(W)}_{{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]}}\ [C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W),E]^{\beta\gamma}\ \xrightarrow{[1,\varphi]^{*}}\ \pi_{0}^{\beta\gamma}(E)

is bijective for every orthogonal
C
-space
E
.
The identity of
C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)
represents a tautological class

[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]\ \in\ [C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W),B_{\gl}\beta]^{\Pi}

that satisfies

[1,\varphi]^{*}(\res^{C\times O(W)}_{{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}])\ =\ \llbracket 1,\varphi\rrbracket\ =\ \gamma^{*}(u_{\beta})

in
\pi_{0}^{\beta\gamma}(B_{\gl}\beta)
.

Now we let
\tau
be any
C
-global transformation from
B_{\gl}\beta
to
E
.
Its naturality properties yield the relations

\displaystyle[1,\varphi]^{*}(\res^{C\times O(W)}_{{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]}

\displaystyle(\tau_{\Pi,C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]))

\displaystyle=\ \tau_{\beta\gamma,\ast}([1,\varphi]^{*}(\res^{C\times O(W)}_{{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]))

\displaystyle=\ \tau_{\beta\gamma,\ast}(\gamma^{*}(u_{\beta}))\ =\ \gamma^{*}(\tau_{\beta,\ast}(u_{\beta}))\ .

Since the composite
[1,\varphi]^{*}\circ\res^{C\times O(W)}_{{\mathcal{S}}[\varphi]}

is bijective by the induction isomorphism (
A.8
),
this relation shows that—and how—the class

\tau_{\Pi,C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]

is determined by the class
\tau_{\beta,\ast}(u_{\beta})
.

Now we consider another compact Lie group
G
, a continuous homomorphism
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
,
a finite
G
-CW-complex
A
, and a class
x\in[A,B_{\gl}\beta]^{\alpha}
.
We represent
x
by a continuous
G
-map
f\colon A\longrightarrow\alpha^{\flat}((B_{\gl}\beta)(W))

for some
G
-representation
W
.
If
A=\emptyset
, then
[A,E]^{\alpha}
has only one element, and there is nothing to show.
If
A
is nonempty, then also
(B_{\gl}\beta)(W)
is nonempty, and there exists
a linear isometric embedding
\varphi\colon V\longrightarrow W
.

We let
\rho\colon G\longrightarrow O(W)
classify the
G
-action on
W
.
Then
(\alpha,\rho)\colon G\longrightarrow C\times O(W)
is a homomorphism over
C
,
in the sense that
\Pi\circ(\alpha,\rho)=\alpha
.
The
G
-action on
\alpha^{\flat}(C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W))=\alpha^{\flat}((B_{\gl}\beta)(W))

is obtained from the
(C\times O(W))
-action
by restriction along
(\alpha,\rho)\colon G\longrightarrow(C\times O(W))
.
So

f\ :\ A\ \longrightarrow\ (\alpha,\rho)^{*}(C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W))

is
G
-equivariant.
Moreover, the tautological relation

x\ =\ [f]\ =\ f^{*}((\alpha,\rho)^{*}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}])

holds, i.e.,
x
is the image of the tautological class under the composite

[C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W),B_{\gl}\beta]^{\Pi}\ \xrightarrow{(\alpha,\rho)^{*}}\ [(\alpha,\rho)^{*}(C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)),B_{\gl}\beta]^{\alpha}\ \xrightarrow{\ f^{*}\ }\ [A,B_{\gl}\beta]^{\alpha}\ .

The naturality properties of the
C
-global transformation
\tau
yield

\displaystyle\tau_{\alpha,A}(x)\

\displaystyle=\ \tau_{\alpha,A}(f^{*}((\alpha,\rho)^{*}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]))

\displaystyle=\ f^{*}((\alpha,\rho)^{*}(\tau_{\Pi,C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]))\ .

We argued above that the class

\tau_{\Pi,C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}[\Id_{C\times_{\beta}{\mathbf{L}}(V,W)}]

is determined by the class
\tau_{\beta,\ast}(u_{\beta})
.
So also
\tau_{\alpha,A}(x)
is determined by the class
\tau_{\beta,\ast}(u_{\beta})
.
This completes the proof of uniqueness.
∎

In the remainder of this appendix,
we investigate the class of
coinduced

C
-global spaces.
For these objects, the
C
-global information is determined,
in the precise sense of Proposition
A.15
,
by the global information of the underlying orthogonal space, i.e., after forgetting the
C
-action.
This class of objects is relevant for our purposes because
for
C=\Gal({\mathbb{C}}/{\mathbb{R}})
, the
C
-global spaces
{\mathbf{BUP}}
,
{\mathbf{U}}
and
\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathbf{KR}})

are coinduced,
see Theorems
B.22
and
B.47
.

Proposition A.13
.

Let
C
be a compact Lie group.
Let
\phi\colon A\longrightarrow B
be a morphism of orthogonal
C
-spaces
that is a global equivalence of underlying orthogonal spaces after
forgetting the
C
-action. Then for every free
C
-space
E
,
the morphism
\phi\times E\colon A\times E\longrightarrow B\times E
is a
C
-global equivalence.

Proof.

Product with any topological space preserves global equivalences
of orthogonal spaces by
[
24
, Proposition 1.1.9 (vii)]
.
Since the underlying morphism of
\phi
is a global equivalence,
so is the underlying morphism of
\phi\times E
.
To show that
\phi\times E
is a
C
-global equivalence, it thus remains
to solve the lifting property from the defining property
[
25
, Definition A.2]

of
C
-global equivalences for all non-trivial continuous homomorphisms

\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
.
But if
\alpha
is non-trivial, then for every
G
-representation
V
, the space

\alpha^{\flat}((B\times E)(V))^{G}\ =\ \alpha^{\flat}(B(V))^{G}\times E^{\alpha(G)}

is empty, because because
C
acts freely on
E
.
Hence there are no lifting problems to solve for
\alpha
, which completes the
proof that
\phi\times E
is a
C
-global equivalence.
∎

In the following we shall write
\llbracket-,-\rrbracket^{C}
for the set of morphisms
in the
C
-global homotopy category, i.e., the localization of
the category of orthogonal
C
-spaces at the class of
C
-global equivalences.
We let
EC
be a universal free
C
-space, i.e., a free
C
-CW-complex
whose underlying space is contractible.
And we shall write
p\colon EC\longrightarrow*
for the unique map.

Definition A.14
.

Let
C
be a compact Lie group.
An orthogonal
C
-space
X
is
coinduced
if for every orthogonal
C
-space
A
the map

\llbracket A\times p,X\rrbracket^{C}\ :\ \llbracket A,X\rrbracket^{C}\ \longrightarrow\ \llbracket A\times EC,X\rrbracket^{C}

induced by
A\times p\colon A\times EC\longrightarrow A
is bijective.

Proposition A.15
.

Let
X
and
Y
be coinduced orthogonal
C
-spaces.

(i)

For every morphism of orthogonal
C
-spaces
\phi\colon A\longrightarrow B

that is a global equivalence of underlying orthogonal spaces after
forgetting the
C
-action, the map

\llbracket\phi,X\rrbracket^{C}\ :\ \llbracket B,X\rrbracket^{C}\ \longrightarrow\ \llbracket A,X\rrbracket^{C}

is bijective.

(ii)

Let
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
be a morphism that is a global equivalence
of underlying orthogonal spaces after forgetting the
C
-action.
Then
f
is a
C
-global equivalence.

Proof.

(i) Because the underlying morphism of
\phi
is a global equivalence
and the
C
-action on
EC
is free, the morphism

\phi\times EC\colon A\times EC\longrightarrow B\times EC
is a
C
-global equivalence by
Proposition
A.13
.
So the lower horizontal map in the following commutative diagram is bijective:

\textstyle{\llbracket B,X\rrbracket^{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket\phi,X\rrbracket^{C}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket B\times p,X\rrbracket^{C}}
\textstyle{\llbracket A,X\rrbracket^{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket A\times p,X\rrbracket^{C}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\llbracket B\times EC,X\rrbracket^{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket\phi\times EC,X\rrbracket^{C}}
\textstyle{\llbracket A\times EC,X\rrbracket^{C}}

The vertical maps are bijective because
X
is coinduced, so
also
\llbracket\phi,X\rrbracket^{C}
is bijective, as claimed.

(ii)
Since
X
is coinduced, the map

\llbracket f,X\rrbracket^{C}\colon\llbracket Y,X\rrbracket^{C}\longrightarrow\llbracket X,X\rrbracket^{C}

is bijective by part (i).
So there is a morphism
g\colon Y\longrightarrow X

in the
C
-global homotopy category such that
gf=\Id_{X}
.
Then

\llbracket f,Y\rrbracket(fg)\ =\ fgf\ =\ f\ =\ \llbracket f,Y\rrbracket(\Id_{Y})\ .

Since
Y
is coinduced, the map

\llbracket f,Y\rrbracket^{C}\colon\llbracket Y,Y\rrbracket^{C}\longrightarrow\llbracket X,Y\rrbracket^{C}

is bijective, again by part (i), so
fg=\Id_{Y}
. Hence
f
is an isomorphism in the

C
-global homotopy category, and thus a
C
-global equivalence.
∎

An orthogonal
C
-space
X
is fibrant in the
C
-global model structure
of
[
5
, Theorem A.20]

if and only if the following condition holds, see
[
5
, Definition A.13]
:
for every continuous homomorphism of compact Lie groups
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C

and every linear isometric embedding of
G
-representations

\varphi\colon V\longrightarrow W
such that
G
acts faithfully on
V
,
the map

\alpha^{\flat}(X(\varphi))^{G}\ :\ \alpha^{\flat}(X(V))^{G}\ \longrightarrow\ \alpha^{\flat}(X(W))^{G}

is a weak equivalence.
We will call such orthogonal
C
-spaces
C
-fibrant
.

Proposition A.16
.

A
C
-fibrant orthogonal
C
-space
X
is coinduced if and only if
the morphism of orthogonal
C
-spaces

\map(p,X)\ :\ X\ \longrightarrow\ \map(EC,X)

is a
C
-global equivalence.

Proof.

The adjoint functors
(-\times EC,\map(EC,-))
are a Quillen functor
pair for the
C
-global model structure, so they induce an adjoint pair
of derived functors at the level of the
C
-global homotopy category.
The left adjoint
-\times EC
is fully homotopical, i.e., it preserves
arbitrary
C
-global equivalences, for example by Proposition
A.13
;
so it descends to the
C
-global homotopy category, and the descended functor is the derived
left adjoint. Since
X
is
C
-fibrant, the orthogonal
C
-space
\map(EC,X)

models the total right derived functor of
\map(EC,-)
on
X
.
The following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\llbracket A,X\rrbracket^{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\llbracket A,\map(p,X)\rrbracket^{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\llbracket A\times p,X\rrbracket^{C}}
\textstyle{\llbracket A,\map(EC,X)\rrbracket^{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
adjunction
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ \llbracket A\times EC,X\rrbracket^{C}}

So
X
is coinduced if and only if for every orthogonal
C
-space
A
,
the left vertical map
\llbracket A,\map(p,X)\rrbracket^{C}
is bijective. This happens if and
only if
\map(p,X)
is an isomorphism in the
C
-global homotopy category,
which is equivalent to
\map(p,X)
being a
C
-global equivalence.
∎

If
X
is an orthogonal space and
G
a compact Lie group,
the
underlying
G
-space
of
X
is the
G
-space

X({\mathcal{U}}_{G})\ =\ \colim_{V\in s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})}X(V)\ ;

the colimit is formed over the poset
s({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
, under inclusion,
of finite-dimensional
G
-subrepresentations of the chosen complete
G
-universe
{\mathcal{U}}_{G}
.
If
X
is an orthogonal
C
-space, then the
C
-action on
X
induces a continuous

C
-action on
X({\mathcal{U}}_{G})
that commutes with the
G
-action, so
X({\mathcal{U}}_{G})

becomes a
(C\times G)
-space.
We write
\alpha^{\flat}(X({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))
for the
G
-space obtained by
restriction of scalars along
(\alpha,\Id)\colon G\longrightarrow C\times G
.

By
[
5
, Proposition 3.5]
,
a morphism
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
between closed orthogonal
C
-spaces
is a
C
-global equivalence if and only if for every
continuous homomorphism
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
of compact Lie groups,
the map

\alpha^{\flat}(f({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))^{G}\ :\ \alpha^{\flat}(X({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))^{G}\ \longrightarrow\ \alpha^{\flat}(Y({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))^{G}

is a weak equivalence.

Proposition A.17
.

Let
X
be an orthogonal
C
-space whose underlying orthogonal space is closed.
Then the following conditions are equivalent.

(a)

The orthogonal
C
-space
X
is coinduced.

(b)

For every continuous homomorphism
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
of compact Lie groups,
the map

\alpha^{\flat}(\map(p,X({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))^{G}\ :\ \alpha^{\flat}(X({\mathcal{U}}_{G}))^{G}\ \longrightarrow\ \alpha^{\flat}(\map(EC,X({\mathcal{U}}_{G})))^{G}=\map^{G}(\alpha^{*}(EC),\alpha^{\flat}(X({\mathcal{U}}_{G})))

is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

We start with a preliminary reduction step.
By choosing an acyclic cofibration
X\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
with fibrant target in the

C
-global model structure, we can assume without loss of generality in both parts
that
X
is not only closed, but also
C
-fibrant.
We let
\alpha\colon G\longrightarrow C
be a continuous homomorphism,
and we let
V
be a faithful
G
-subrepresentation of the universe
{\mathcal{U}}_{G}
.
Because
X
is
C
-fibrant and closed,
the left vertical map in the commutative diagram

(A.18)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.85507pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.76985pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 156.13438pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
